drawn, where 1,088 were before); and a value or a state's word too long for its column is drawn cut with an ellipsis,

on the button that holds the focus in an old message box; the four focus borders gone from the sheet) passed its gate
(1271 passed, 3 skipped) and the display-marked run. **The pictures** were then taken with the real window hidden on a
scratch settings file and an empty scratch store: 47 of every screen in light and dark, and the same 47 at a forced
text size of 150 %. Four readers went through all of them against the "before" set and found FAULTS OF THE NEW LOOK,
each traced to its cause: every hyphen inside a word had become as wide as a digit ("cross - validation"), because the
equal-width-digits feature of the typeface replaces 61 glyphs, not only the digits; the "?" in a help badge could not
be read on its ring; lists, the help viewer and bare scrolling areas had no outline of the look's (none at all in
dark); the cells of every filled list and table had moved 2 px. **Task 10g** (`fc8c062` to `88a4ea3`, then a fix round
to `bb75ad2`; brief written by the controller, `stage-1/briefs/task-10g-brief.md` in the ledger) fixed them: digits of
equal width only for lists, tables, their headers, the progress read-outs and the run slot (deviation 37); the badge's
ring faint again; the frames named in the sheet (deviation 38); the focus frame's margins for the ring alone; and a
latent failure of a theme change on a top-level list. **The last gate, at `bb75ad2`: 1279 passed, 3 skipped,
5 deselected, 203 warnings, 15 min 55 s; the display-marked run 3 passed.** The pictures were made again on that head
and are kept in the ledger's `stage-1/pictures/`: `now/` and `now-text150/` (47 each), `compare-before-now.html` (94
side by side), `for-the-owner/` (twelve before-and-now pairs), beside the "before" set. **A measurement in the real
window** (Task 10's review raised it; a second agent checked the measurement): the small style as the APPLICATION's
style and the follower as a filter on every event put Python into every repaint, which never entered it before; with
nothing running that costs under a millisecond a repaint, but WHILE A FIGURE IS DRAWN ON THE WORKER THE WINDOW
STUTTERS (about three repaints of over a tenth of a second per 13-parameter figure, the longest 0.86 s, where none took
over 4 ms); simulation and training themselves do not cause it; a shorter interpreter switch interval was tried and
rejected (it makes other cases worse). **Ruled: Task 10f**, before Task 13: the application wears the toolkit's own
Fusion, the Python-written overrides serve only the widgets that need them, the follower decides from the
application's focus signal and what was pressed last (deviation 36); a feasibility probe (two probes, a design, a
skeptic) is running, its reports go to `stage-1/reviews/task-10f-*.md`, and its brief is written from them. **For the
owner with the pictures:** locked tabs cannot be told from open ones at a glance (greyed text is darker by design;
remedy on offer), hint text in an empty field reads as a value, a greyed main button looks pressable when alone; the
old layout at 150 % text is awkward everywhere (the left column is a fixed 420 px) but nothing is unusable. **For
later dispatches:** Tasks 14, 15 and 17 ask the lettering function for the tabular form where numbers stand in columns
or change in place; a font handed to a widget after the sheet has dressed it is dropped at the next change of sheet
(give it at build); a frameless scrolling area switches its frame off at build. **Next, in order:** the owner's
remarks (Task 13 waits for them); Task 10f when its design is in; Tasks 11b and 12 (E12) may be built meanwhile; then
13 to 18, 19.

**TASK 10f, THE STUTTER (2026-10-08; `3ef959a` to `d78c923`; the ledger's entries "Task 10 (TWO-1)" and "Task 10f"
have every figure, and `stage-1/reviews/task-10f-*.md` every report).** Three rulings in a row, each made on a
measurement or a probe: (1) the style and the follower off the application — probed, NOT built (three ways it could
end the process); (2) a lighter reduction of the style — probed, NOT built (it halves the waiting and costs five
guarantees); (3) BUILT: **the main window's class handles its events through a one-line method in Python that only
calls the base class** (`core/gui/main_window.py`), so the toolkit's repaint, which runs inside the window's handling of
its update request, keeps the interpreter for its whole length. Measured in the real window, shown off-screen, with the
method in the code: one whole repaint beside plain Python takes 8 to 18 ms on every page where 196 to 1,107 ms stood;
beside the real figure work the share of repaints over a tenth of a second falls from 2 % to 0.1 %; the run loses
nothing measurable. **Its price, and what was built against it:** the binding then drops an error raised by Python
code the toolkit calls from inside that handling, so the window's own drawing code reports its errors itself: the new
`core/gui/reported.py` (a guard: the error handed to the window's hook once per method, kept as text, repeats counted,
the stock answer as fallback) on the ten methods the toolkit calls while the window is drawn or laid out (the style's
three, the ring's paint, the follower's filter, two old paint events, the figure stack's resize, two pages' show
events); a standing scan in `tests/test_look.py` that lets no new such method into `core/gui` without it (twenty-one
method names; the next two devices as their briefs dictate them are caught); and a new autouse fixture in
`tests/conftest.py` (`_drawing_code_raises_nothing`: it runs for every test, a per-test check, not session scope) that
FAILS a test during which a guarded method raised. Confirmed by measurement on a stock window
with the same method: no slot of the main window can fail silently because of it, a box opened beneath it does not
stall a working thread, a failure in the close handler is still reported, the order of events is unchanged. **Accepted
and written down in the code:** an error inside a drawing LIBRARY's own Python code in the main window (the live
simulation's plot) shows no box; the figure pop-out is left without the method for that reason; a change of page or
tab, a resize, the pointer and the keyboard are only about halved while a figure is drawn. **The last gate, at
`d78c923`: 1297 passed, 3 skipped, 5 deselected, 203 warnings, 16 min 0 s; the display-marked run 3 passed (at
`8764a06`).** Deviation 36 in the plan's head and the design's section 8 is rewritten to this. **Carried to Task 13's
dispatch:** a small tests-only commit first (the scan judges the real tree before its in-memory checks; a guarded
method in the live view's class passes); every drawing method of Tasks 13 and 14 carries the guard, and both briefs'
import rules let `core.gui.reported` in; the last test Task 13's brief dictates expects an error to come OUT of the
icon's pixmap, which under the guard it does not; the indicator's painting gets its `try`/`finally`; Tasks 14, 15 and
17 ask the lettering for digits of equal width; the shared question's class (Task 18) does NOT get the method. **For
the audit's list, older than this stage and found on the way:** an error raised by the callable of a zero-delay single
shot with a context object is silent in this binding (`core/gui/panels/record_view.py:200`); a tree item's less-than
that raises during a sort ended a probe's process (`core/gui/widgets/artifact_table.py:196` is such a method; not
followed further); a failure in the main window's close handler before "Don't quit" is read closes the window with a
run still going. **Owed at the stage's exit:** `CLAUDE.md` counts the standing guards of the tests without the two
this stage added (the file choosers', session-wide; the drawing code's, a per-test check) and is corrected only with
the owner's approval of the wording; `docs/guide/testing.md` lists them without the same two, and whether that page
is corrected at the exit or left for the stage that rewrites the guide is not ruled (the carry list, Task 19).
**Next:** the owner's remarks on the pictures; then Task 13.

**THE CONSOLE RETRAIN IS STOPPED AND THE ORDER OF WORK HAS CHANGED (owner,
2026-10-05).** The retrain started from PowerShell on 1 October reached a COMPLETE simulation cache (10,000 batches;
gate 3 passed, 40.1 % against P = 37.9 %) and 62 epochs of the fit (61–63 minutes each; the capture's last count is 61,
one behind) before the owner stopped it on
5 October (~10:25, Ctrl-C; no PRISM process afterwards, the card free, no posterior written). The owner never wanted
it run from the console, runs PRISM from the window, accepts losing the fit, and does not require the finished
simulations to be reused. **New order: (1) a complete UI/UX redesign of the window, in another chat; (2) then an audit
of the whole code for bugs and mistakes, in another chat; (3) then the retrain, started and watched from the window.**
The two hand-off prompts are `docs/superpowers/prompts/2026-10-05-ui-ux-redesign.md` and
`docs/superpowers/prompts/2026-10-05-code-audit.md`; both start from
`docs/superpowers/notes/2026-10-05-the-stopped-retrain-and-the-window-route.md` — what the run measured, what the window
cannot do or does unsafely on a days-long run (read from the code by four readers, three of them each followed by a
refuter, then the whole note checked against the capture and the code by six more; NOTHING there was executed, and
none of it is a decision of the owner's), and the audit's leads. **Kept on disk, to be left alone unless the owner says
otherwise:** `retrain_prior__20261001T222935`, the cache `Artifacts/simulations/eda5c2a1e468` (complete, 20,480,000
rows), `retrain_band__20261001T222941`, `retrain_mask__20261001T223011`, and the console capture
`C:\Users\J\prism-retrain-console.log` (263 MB, UTF-16; the only copy of the first process's `[fisher]` lines for the
rotation stored in that cache). **The headline findings.** *From the run:* with no VRAM ceiling the first process died
of out-of-memory at batch 237; at 8 GiB the longest batches reserved 13.4–14.8 GiB and went through on Windows'
overcommit; at 6 GiB nothing failed in 9,500 batches (the longest, n_fine 299,998, reserved 11.81 GiB), and the restart
loop put around the command never had to act — and the window's ceiling opens at 0 on every launch unless
`PRISM_VRAM_CEILING_GIB` is set in the environment the window starts in (the field then opens at that value and the
variable wins), and the window has nothing that restarts a failed run; the fit has no checkpoint, so any stop loses it;
`[patho] run total` counts one process (30,847 of 20,480,000, 0.151 %, summed by hand); `[winsor]` read 0.083 %; a
full-size epoch took 63 minutes where the runbook's ×500 rule gives 52. *From the code, unexecuted:* the window has no
diagnostics at all (gates 2 and 4 and the stratified SBC are command-line only); its narrowing round always trains at
128 × 8; its network boxes reopen at 128 × 8 on every launch and are outside the cache's identity, so a relaunch can fit
the wrong network with no warning; only a one-setting near miss raises the new-run dialog; it keeps no file of a run's
lines (a 5,000-line pane; `log.txt` only when the record commits); Cancel during a fit lands at an epoch boundary (the
screen says a minute); the close dialog defaults to "Cancel task & quit"; and "Quit anyway" during a fit probably
deletes the record at the end (unverified). `docs/guide/` is unchanged by all this: `retrain.md` is still the console
runbook (the audit chat rewrites it as the window route), and the page errors found are listed in the note's part 3C.
The standing rule that follows is in `CLAUDE.md` ("Long PRISM runs … are started from the window"). No file under
`core/` or `tests/` has changed since `3ec5bf6`: `771a460` and every commit after it are documents. One of them,
`09557cf`, edited `README.md` and the guide's `command-line.md`, `science.md` and `testing.md`, which the docs suite and
the source scan read; no fast gate has run since `771a460`, but the two suites that read those pages
(`tests/test_docs.py`, `tests/test_source_hygiene.py`) passed on 2026-10-05 after the stop (11 passed, 7.6 s). `main` was 25 ahead of `origin/main` (`f89088b`) before today's last documents commit, NOT pushed.
**Later on 2026-10-05 (owner): the redesign chat is to use the plugins installed that day, and the audit also covers
the MATH, against a PDF of the models that the owner will supply.** Both prompts were revised for it, and the math has a
note of its own, `docs/superpowers/notes/2026-10-05-where-the-models-math-lives.md` (one reader's map, not checked by a
second: where each equation is implemented, where it is written more than once, the conventions to pin down, the checks
that do not trust the code, what to ask the owner about the PDF). Nothing in the repository or its archive states the
models today, and the tests pin the code's copies of each equation against each other, never against an independent
statement. **A FINDING from that mapping, REPRODUCED the same day with the program's own functions on the CPU
(`core.forcing`, `core.Helpers.helpers`, `core.SBI.chi`, float64): model time is put on the recording's clock by a
whole-number stride** (`subsample_factor = max(1, round(dt_nd_k / dt_nd_min))`, `core/SBI/pipeline.py:1654-1657`), and
every later step treats the kept points as exactly `dt_exp` apart. So (1) a simulated trace is that of a cell at
`t_scale_hi / stride` (40, 20, 13.3, 10, 8, 6.67, … on the master boxes) while the row is labelled with the continuous
`t_scale` it was drawn at (at 25 the trace is a cell at 20; at 30, a cell at 40), and (2) the drive, built with the
continuous `t_scale` (`core/forcing.py:171`), is detuned from the lock-in that measures the response
(`core/SBI/chi.py:463`). A response equal to its drive, whose true answer is 1, read |χ| = 0.625 after 20 drive cycles
at the master cell's t_scale 3.73 (0.029 after 40), 0.180 at 7.215, 0.168 at 13.9, 0.000 at 25 and at 30 — and 1.000
wherever 40 / t_scale is whole, or when the lock-in is given the true spacing of the kept points. NOT yet shown: an
end-to-end training batch, the size of the effect on a real bundle's response, whether forced mode's features and the
FDT path share it, or what "the model at t_scale" is meant to be (the owner's to say); no fix is designed. If it stands,
the finished cache cannot be trained on (its key does not change with the code), the stopped fit was training on
affected rows, and no calibration on simulated data could have shown it. It may be the cause of the unexplained "wall"
at 32–36 drive cycles in `docs/guide/science.md` (the null at the master cell is at ~39 cycles); that link is an
inference. Two more leads from the mapping are unreproduced: the drive's clock starts before the discarded transient,
so simulated phases carry an offset a bench recording does not; and the solver's float32 step differs between segments
by +0.10 % / −0.39 %. **The plugins** (read from their files, not run): impeccable 4.5.0, ui-ux-pro-max 2.13.0 and
frontend-design are design aids built for web pages and phone apps; none knows Qt, so they serve for direction,
mock-up pages, critique and the design record, not for building the window. impeccable's three hooks run in EVERY chat
on this machine (after each edit and at each stop); it writes `PRODUCT.md`, `DESIGN.md` and `.impeccable/` at the
repository root (git ignores none of them: the owner's call), can edit the project's `.claude/settings.local.json`, and
contacts its website (its draw of design directions, an anonymous usage ping, a version check). ui-ux-pro-max can save a
`design-system/` folder into the repository (unwanted) and its logo and icon generators send prompts to outside services
(no key is set). claude-mem is switched off and claude-mem-cowork cannot run (no Node, not paired; once paired it would
upload prompts and the contents of files): its hook fails harmlessly on every prompt. **Qt Development Skills 1.7.1
(Qt Group) was installed by the owner on the evening of 2026-10-05 and then SWITCHED OFF: its README says that using its
skills or its hosted documentation lookup accepts Qt's AI Services terms, and the owner does not accept them.** Both
prompts say to leave it off. Claude read its installed files only (the design skill, the README, the lookup's
description) and never invoked a skill or called the lookup. What it would have added, and what the redesign chat is
told to do instead: none of the design plugins knows Qt, so what a widget, a layout or a style sheet can do is checked
against Qt's public documentation or the installed PySide6, with a scratch script.
**THE REDESIGN CHAT HAS STARTED (2026-10-05, evening), from `docs/superpowers/prompts/2026-10-05-ui-ux-redesign.md`;
it is at the brainstorming stage and NOTHING under `core/` or `tests/` has changed.** What it has so far is in three
new notes: `docs/superpowers/notes/2026-10-05-the-window-as-read-for-the-redesign.md` (the summary: read it first) and
its two appendices, `…-the-window-as-read-findings.md` (198 entries, ids W001–W198, each with what a person would see,
the second reader's correction and the code it rests on) and `…-the-window-as-read-inventory.md` (the window screen by
screen, what each stage accepts against what the window offers, where a long run could attach, what the tests pin).
**The owner's decisions so far (in chat):** what is disliked — Home ("5 buttons just slapped in the center"), the
cluttered and hard-to-read screens under each tab, getting around the whole program, the progress bars ("cluttered and
not interpretable"), the console text shown by default (people "need to only see what they need to by default"), and
the design language, with no direction in mind; above all "extreme clarity"; **the window may be RELEASED**, so
strangers will open it; **the toolkit stays PySide6 unless an approved design is blocked by it** (then stop and bring
the blockage and the cost); **the scope is the look, layout and flow PLUS everything the engine can already do that the
window does not offer or say, PLUS the small engine changes** (loss per epoch while fitting, a guard so that closing the
window cannot destroy a finished result, every differing setting named); **saving the fit as it goes is to come as a
separate design, to accept or leave.** **How it was read:** ten readers over `core/gui/`, the engine side, the pages,
the walkthrough and the earlier designs; seven second readers told to refute every new fault; two sizing the tests; all
reading, nothing executed. Of the first note's re-checked claims 127 held, 30 held with a correction, 5 could not be
settled by reading; of the 174 entries the second readers took, 167 hold (70 corrected), 1 is wrong, 6 unsettled. The
corrections that matter: only each tab's LEFT column is locked during a run (navigation, figures, the log, Artifacts
reading stay live); most of what the first note lists as missing is a change to the WINDOW ONLY (every stage takes a
name and a note; training and the narrowing round take an epoch ceiling, a cadence, a resume policy, the round its own
network; the calibration a seed; every diagnostic is a public entry writing a record; a run's lines can be written to a
file at the window's pump); during the fit's whole first epoch the progress pane shows nothing and then "no output".
**ONE EXPERIMENT WAS RUN (no card, no store): "Quit anyway" does not do what it says.** With PRISM's own worker, stream
redirect and figure sink in a bare window that exits as `core/gui/__main__.py` does: after the window closes the process
stays alive and the run goes on unseen to its end, then the figure hand-over raises `RuntimeError: Signal source has
been deleted` — in PRISM that hand-over is inside the posterior's writer, which removes its folder on any exception, and
the worker swallows the report (read, not run). "Cancel task & quit" stopped the run at its next line and the process
waited for it to unwind (so the rescue save during generation can finish). With a popped-out figure window open,
closing the main window does not end the program at all. A real check on a scratch store is owed as a walkthrough row.
**Faults that can cost days (all within the chosen scope):** the close dialog's default cancels the run and never says
a fit would be lost (W001); Cancel during a fit is one click with no question; the screen can show one bounds file
while the run uses another (W002, W003, W138); the rotation tick is remembered, and a run without it says nothing
(W008, W142); the narrowing round keeps the earlier observation selected and always trains 128 × 8 (W009); nothing
keeps Windows awake, and closing the launcher's console kills the run (W004, W139); a second window can delete a cache
a run is writing (W137, W140). **The tests:** about 279 of the 322 window tests would pass if only the arrangement
changed under the same names, labels and dialogs; a real redesign breaks many more (the inventory lists the names the
tests reach for and each pin it collides with). **DECIDED BY THE OWNER LATER THAT EVENING, from pictures (three ways of
getting around as wireframes, three looks on one sample screen, each built, critiqued by an independent reader and
revised; all in the chat's scratch folder, nothing published, nothing in the repository) and from questions with their
options laid out — the note's part 1 has each in full:** (1) getting around: **the parts on a rail that never goes
away, Home as a status page, the six inference steps as a chain with a state and a reason each, a strip that follows a
live run**, plus a plain "what do you want to do?" opening for a first-time visitor and a "made from / used by" view in
Records with the next action on a record's page; (2) the look: **"from the scientific page"** — ink on a clear page,
three-rule tables, a marker shape per state with its word, one accent blue, the colour-blind-safe figure colours,
progress on a labelled time axis with a hatched range, journal-style charts, Inter for lettering and a book face for
the program's sentences — plus real system dialogs, stock control shapes and a recent/all switch on the loss curve;
(3) **a long run's settings come from NAMED RUN PLANS** (a small file per kind of run beside the bounds and cell
files; every launch still opens at the built-in values; the window never restores a science setting on its own; the
chi band and drive cannot be in a plan); (4) **the card memory limit moves to Settings, per computer, and IS
remembered** — a reversal of the standing rule for that one setting, answered by showing it before every run and
storing it in each run's record (`CLAUDE.md`'s rule is to be rewritten when it is built); (5) **closing the window
during a run asks and keeps running by default**; the only other answer stops the run, waits until it has stopped,
then closes; "Quit anyway" goes; (6) **after a failure** the run's page says what was saved and offers to continue,
and the window retries by itself a few times in place first (no unattended restart in a fresh program in this
redesign); (7) **vocabulary: the field's terms explained once, plain words for the machinery, one name per thing**
(Records, simulation set, memory limit, training, narrowing round, Model and options); (8) **every check there is gets

**Earlier, 2026-10-01: sbi is UPGRADED to 0.26.1 (owner)** — `ae1b21c`, `750d58f`,
`3ec5bf6`, `771a460`, NOT pushed. A read-only audit (58 agents: an inventory, seven areas, a verifier per finding)
found the upgrade breaks more than trivially, and the owner chose to upgrade properly rather than fall back:
sbi 0.26 renamed the z-scoring check PRISM caps (every training failed at `append_simulations`; its replacement runs
`torch.quantile` over the whole table, which torch refuses beyond 2**24 rows — after a 20.48M-row generation), and its
TARP grows its bins with n_cal (n // 10: 200 at 2,000, a crash below 10) and now scales its references (0.25 left
them raw, so its "joint" test was dominated by f_scale on the master box and T on the tier-1 box). Fixed test-first:
the cap retargeted and resolved at import (an ImportError naming the version if missing), skipped in chi mode,
otherwise a strided ≤200k-row subsample with sbi's own arguments forwarded, clamped to 2**24; TARP's bins fixed at 30
(`config.TARP_NUM_BINS`, recorded as `results.tarp.num_bins`), ATC printed with significant figures; a do-nothing
`tracker` in place of the deprecated `summary_writer`; any error from the fit re-raised as a RuntimeError (the tool
prints FAILED, not "refused"); plain numbers at sbi's door; `train_nn` frees its own ~9.3 GiB copy of the training
data before the fit; skorch 1.4.0 and tabulate 0.10.0 pinned. The two loop behaviours the runbook relies on are
unchanged (read in 0.26.1's source: `--max-epochs N` trains N + 1, the best network is restored only when early
stopping fires; sbi's printed lines are identical). TARP at 30 bins stays weak against a flaw in ONE parameter (a
simulated posterior 3× too narrow in one of 13 read KS p 1.000 under either sbi): the rank tests carry that. TARP
figures from before the upgrade are not comparable (science.md says so). Reviewed by three lenses with a verifier each
(0 Critical; the fixes are `3ec5bf6`, `771a460`). Gate rows below. ~~NEXT: the card check on 0.26.1~~ (PASSED 2026-10-01, the gate table).
**The three pre-retrain decisions are SETTLED (owner, 2026-09-30, in chat),
and the prior's fill is fixed** — commits `2cd8c38`, `0987395`, `6b63d16`, `b2d73c2`, `64109a9` on the local `main`,
NOT pushed (the owner pushes). (a) **The stop point** is now a **budget: four weeks of the card (672 h) for the retrain
and its certification round** — about twice the fit plus ~95 h, so each fit ≤ ~290 h: stop above ~16 s per epoch in the
timing (which now runs ten epochs and also times the 128 × 8 flow; report a 128 × 8 reading above ~7 s), report the
programme total before the run, and in flight stop above ~2.2 h per full-size epoch or ~290 h of fitting. The 740 h
figure rested on parameter scaling; the August fit's ~64 ms per 512-row step says a step is overhead-bound, so the likely
fit is ~110–145 h (upper bound ~340 h; unmeasured on the card). (b) **The masked-probe checks**: pre-flight `probes mask`
at `--num-runs 100`, pass within 37 ± 8, owner decides within ± 12, stop beyond; gate 3 is read when generation ends,
against that audit's reading P: |total − P| ≤ 3 go on, ≤ 5 look closer, > 5 stop the fit (the cache is complete). 37 %
itself is uncertain by ~3 points (smoke-size runs sharing a seed and the fixed probe layout); tier 1 cannot move the
fraction (the drive is fixed non-dimensionally, so the force scale cancels). The smoke gate keeps ± 12. (c) **The prior
screen**: the owner chose to fix the fill in code (not "sweep only", not "count first"). The research found a larger flaw
than the two gaps: the FIFO walk gave each popped point a whole batch of 1,024 steps, so at the defaults only the first
~72 census points were ever walked and ~20–25 % of the prior's weight sat in ~72 tight clumps (three archived summer
priors: 77–78 components; a CPU emulation of the old walk: 75 components, 26 % tight weight, 34.5 % of draws near the
clumps). `0987395` + `b2d73c2`: a shared `Prior._walk` draws parents at random from every kept point, steps each once,
keeps only in-box finite steps, stops at `n_max` or a round that keeps nothing, returns the set sorted (a user model's
too), and logs `[prior] stability sweep: …` and `[prior] fill: …`; the census now integrates the full stability duration
(`t_global_scale` removed). Emulated at production size: 1 or 2 broad components, no tight weight, near-uniform over
the box; HDBSCAN often logs `No clusters found. Defaulting to 1 cluster` (a warning, expected on the master and tier-1
boxes). (d) **The solver question** (owner, same chat): the compiled Euler step ignores `state_dep_drift` by design —
each model's compiled step carries its own noise law; `2cd8c38` pins the registry flag to each compiled step on the CPU
(Nadrowski and Hopf, live noise) and corrects `euler_compiled`'s docstring; the misleading name ("drift" for noise) is
on the open list. Guide pages re-stamp only when re-checked against new code (owner, 2026-09-30): `science`, `window`,
`command-line`, `architecture`, `retrain`, `testing` now read `b2d73c2`; the rest keep `30ff8a3`. The gate table has the
fast gate; the card smoke gate on this code is pre-flight step 1 of the retrain.

(3) ~~The owner's pre-flight step 5 again, step 6 and the run~~ — **THE RETRAIN STARTED 2026-10-01 ~15:52** (the owner, from PowerShell with the console capture to `C:\Users\J\prism-retrain-console.log`, UTF-16). Every opening line as the runbook expects: the chi banner (12 slots, 72 features), the tier-1 bounds and rescale order, both `[tier1]` lines, `[budget] 10,000 batches x 2,048 rows`; the Fisher over **8/8** operating points, spread 2.21e+16; the cache `Artifacts/simulations/eda5c2a1e468` (every 50 batches, ~10.3 GiB, 1,532 GiB free); `[mem]` batch 1 0.58 GiB allocated, 0.91 reserved. First commit at batch 50 (16:00, 53 MB). Pace ~5.2 s per batch (75 batches in 391 s), so generation should end ~06:20 on 2026-10-02: then gate 3 (the run total against P = 37.9 %, 3/5 points) and the fit (projected ~112 h; in-flight limits 2.2 h per epoch, 290 h). **The first process DIED at batch 237 (16:23:28, `*** FAILED ***`), nothing lost**: batch 237 [t_scale=7.215, n_fine=198,874, N_points=32,479] hit `torch.AcceleratorError: CUDA error: out of memory` in its first chi probe call; every split (1024/512/256 rows) and every recovery call failed the same way (`empty_cache`, `mem_get_info`, the RNG restore, a 256-row model construction), through all four whole-batch attempts (15/60/180 s waits), with nvidia-smi at 15.9 of 16.3 GB; the rescue save then committed 200 → 236 (`batches_done: 236`). Larger geometries had fit earlier (batch 176, n_fine 289,540, three segments). A read-only investigation (three angles, one synthesis; the GPU untouched) judged the most likely cause, at medium-low confidence, to be FRAGMENTATION INSIDE the batch: at the top of the two-segment range the solution (~5.0 GiB rounded), segment (~2.5) and drive (~1.6) buffers fall in different roundup classes, so freed blocks are not reused exactly and the first probe's solution asks the driver for a fresh block, which WDDM overcommits into shared memory instead of refusing; the overcommit surfaces at a launch as AcceleratorError, which the caching allocator's release-and-retry path never sees, and every recovery call then fails too, so only a new process recovers. Ruled out: the CUDA graph cache (bounded, a few MiB per entry) and any device tensor growing across batches. Unmeasured: reserved memory across batches (the only `[mem]` line was batch 1). **Resume (the owner's choice, 2026-10-01): no code change; in the PowerShell session `PRISM_VRAM_CEILING_GIB=8` (splits batches planned above 8 GiB into 1,024-row chunks, ~9 % of batches, generation up to ~1.3× slower; not in the cache identity), `PRISM_MEM_LOG_EVERY=1` (a peak allocated/reserved line per batch, to confirm or refute the cause), `TORCH_SHOW_CPP_STACKTRACES=1`; and a restart loop of at most 5 tries that re-runs the same `--resume require` command only when it exits 1 with `*** FAILED` and `out of memory` in the capture's last 80 lines.** Owed soon after, between two processes at a commit (no identity change): fail fast when an AcceleratorError OOM leaves `empty_cache`/`mem_get_info` failing (skip the futile ladder, re-raise distinctly; the rescue save still runs); possibly `set_per_process_memory_fraction` as an argument, after a card measurement (it would turn the spill into a clean OutOfMemoryError the ladders handle, and make `garbage_collection_threshold` active); swap `empty_cache` and the graph drop in `_release_device_memory`. The `[patho]` run total covers only the last process (sum the per-process lines). **RESUMED 2026-10-01 ~16:39 with that plan, and past batch 237**: `Reusing the Fisher rotation … (236/10000 batches)`, the stored eigenvalues carried (spread 2.21e+16), `[checkpoint] resuming at batch 236/10000`, nothing set aside, no `[fisher]` line. Per-batch memory: batch 237 peak allocated 7.15 GiB / reserved 10.29; 239 (n_fine 232,000) 7.62 / 11.23; 241 5.65 / 8.30; small batches 0.83–2.7 / 1.3–4.2 — large batches reserve ~45 % above what they allocate (fragmentation inside the batch, as the investigation judged), and reserved drops straight back after each one (no floor building across batches). No graph-capture fallback. Watch the longest batches (n_fine ~280–290k). **Through batch 300 (committed 16:48:50)**: 6 of 62 batches reserved ≥ 12 GiB; the largest, batch 292 (n_fine 291,525, about the top of the box's range), peaked at 9.50 GiB allocated / 13.68 reserved and went through; a one-minute nvidia-smi sample read 1.37–15.74 GB of 16.30, so at the longest geometry about 0.55 GB stays free. The 8 GiB ceiling bounds only the simulator's own buffers (the probe stage's other tensors and the allocator's rounding come on top of it). No out-of-memory split, no graph fallback. Advice given: keep the desktop light (no GPU-heavy programs) until generation ends; if the loop restarts more than once at the same place, the next step is a lower ceiling (6 GiB) at a commit boundary. **Then batch 386 (n_fine 297,056; the fine grid is capped at 300,000) peaked at 10.10 GiB allocated / 14.80 reserved** -- more than the card leaves beside the desktop and the context (~1.3 GiB, the one-minute floor), so it went through only on WDDM's overcommit, the mode the first process died in; 7 of 78 batches reserved ≥ 12 GiB; 8.46 s/batch over 309–386 (5.2 before the ceiling and the per-batch memory lines). With ~9,500 batches (~22 h, mostly overnight) left, a 5-try loop could run out. **Owner's choice (2026-10-01): restart at a commit boundary with `PRISM_VRAM_CEILING_GIB=6` and the loop at 20 tries.** Done: commit at batch 500 at 17:12:41, Ctrl-C under the pipe (the old process had reached 505; those 5 batches are redone), the loop restarted and resumed at `[checkpoint] resuming at batch 500/10000`, the stored rotation reused (spread 2.21e+16), no `[fisher]` line. The 6 GiB plan splits a 2,048-row probe call above n_fine ≈ 196,000 (8 GiB: ≈ 262,000) and the longest into 512-row chunks instead of 1,024, about 1.6 GiB less allocated at the top of the range by the planner's own arithmetic; the full-batch drive and result are outside its chunking and stay. **Measured over batches 508–587**: none reserved ≥ 12 GiB (the largest 9.87, batch 578); the longest, batch 585 (n_fine 260,466), peaked at 8.17 GiB allocated / 8.55 reserved, against 9.2–9.4 / 13.4 for n_fine 268–280k under 8 GiB -- the split batches' reserved now sits close to what they allocate. Pace 9.01 s/batch (8.46 under 8 GiB), so generation ends around 17:00 on 2 Oct. **GENERATION ENDED 2026-10-02 18:46** (the third process: 9,500 batches in 25 h 32 min; 9.7 s/batch over the night, 10.2 over the last 113; no failure, restart or out-of-memory split after the 6 GiB restart; the longest batch the box draws, n_fine 299,998 at batch 4948, peaked at 10.43 GiB allocated / 11.81 reserved, and no batch reserved ≥ 12 GiB). **Gate 3 PASSES**: `[chi] masked probes: 57,703,562 of 143,831,040 (40.1%) over 10000 batches, every committed batch of the simulation cache; per batch 16.5-92.9%, median 37.8%` against P = 37.9 %, D = 2.2 points (≤ 3: the fit goes on); the per-batch median matches the audit. `[patho]`, summed over the committed batches of the three processes (the third's run total plus the first's and the second's cumulative counts through their last committed batch, 235 and 499): 727 of 483,328 + 875 of 540,672 + 29,245 of 19,456,000 = **30,847 of 20,480,000 (0.151 %)**, 30,825 non-finite, 0 constant, 22 over 1e+15. `[checkpoint] complete: 10000 batches in …\eda5c2a1e468` -- keep the cache (`ablation` reads it). The fit then began to assemble its rows (host RAM 12.8 GB working set, 29 GB free). `[winsor] clipped 852,885 of 1,024,000,000 summary elements (0.083%) to their per-column 0.1%/99.9% percentiles` (20,480,000 rows x 50 summary columns; under the few tenths of a percent expected). **THE FIT IS RUNNING, read 2026-10-05 09:13**: the same process (started 2026-10-01 17:14), host memory flat at 24.7 GB working set since 2 Oct (17 GB free), the card at 2.9 GB and ~19 % (the step is overhead-bound, as the timing found); sbi's counter reads 60, last written to the capture at 09:07:13, so 60 epochs in at most 62 h 19 min since generation ended: **at most ~62 min per epoch (about 1.04 h; the stop is ~2.2 h)**, against the timing's projection of ~52 min. At that pace the budget's ~130 epochs would end around 8 Oct; the 290 h stop falls on 14 Oct ~20:45. No other line since `[winsor]`. **Timed**: count 60 reached the capture at 09:07:13 and count 61 at 10:10:14, **63.0 min for the epoch (1.05 h; the stop is ~2.2 h)**, in line with the 62 min average. Read the pace from the capture's own write time and its latest count at two moments, as the runbook says: a background watcher is stopped by the app after 30 minutes now (the four-count timer was, having measured nothing), so a watch over days has to be a reading on request or a scheduled one. **OWNER'S DECISION 2026-10-05: abandon this console run and redo the retrain from the WINDOW** -- the owner never wanted it run from the console, wants to work in the app again, accepts losing the fit (62 epochs, ~63 h) and does not require the finished simulation cache to be reused (a fresh simulation is acceptable; the cache `eda5c2a1e468` stays on disk unless the owner deletes it). The console process (PID 7152) was still running when this was written; the owner stops it (Ctrl-C in its PowerShell window, which ends the restart loop too). **STOPPED by the owner 2026-10-05 ~10:25**: no PRISM process at 10:31, the card at 1.9 GiB, no `Artifacts/posteriors/`; the capture's last count is 61, and 62 epochs had finished: a count reaches the file only when sbi prints the next, so the readings above ("counter reads 60", "count 61") are each one behind — the timed 63.0 minutes is the 62nd epoch, and the average over the 61 before it is at most 61.3 minutes. Gate 3's reading above belongs to that cache. The runbook (`docs/guide/retrain.md`) is console-only; a read-only check of the window route (which settings the training tab reaches, how a days-long run behaves there, which post-fit checks exist only on the command line) was started the same day -- its result decides what the window route still needs. (Moot since the stop:) when the fit ends, `[prism] posterior retrain__<id>`, the loss curve, then `validate`, `identifiability rotation`, `ablation`, the four `sbc` runs and gates 1, 2 and 4. store kind, `fdt`, whose records are PROGRESSIVE — the folder and a first manifest exist from the
moment a run starts, `refresh()` rewrites them as it goes, and a cancel or a crash keeps the folder
marked unfinished, while the six ordinary kinds still lose theirs on any exception (a mode on
`ArtifactWriter`, never a second writer). Both analyses are public entries writing named records
with provenance (the cell, the units and the resolved bounds file by path and SHA-256), a recorded
seed and their numbers in `data.h5`. The sweep is split into one record per swept parameter,
carrying its done/failed counts, with an all-failed refusal — and an all-failed first sweep no
longer costs the second: the temperature sweep runs regardless and the study refuses only if both
measured nothing (P77). Two pre-spend refusals — the normalisation constant before the first
campaign, the band once the grid is built and before the driven campaign — plus a third for a
diverged spectrum, and the off-grid fix (probes the spectrum cannot supply come back blank and are
counted, not fabricated). The five screens' builder refusals reach the yellow box, `_config_error`
is gone, and the fix-hint table understands screens as well as the six inference tabs. The pickers
offer earlier runs, and the figure watcher points at the record's `figures/`. `fdt` and `crossval`
take `--store-root`, with the three behaviours that keyed off the flag reworked (smoke's throwaway
root is its own `temp_store_root` property), and `--seed` (P79) — and, since the review, `--name`
and `--note`. The tidy-up offers loose files inside a kind directory and a legacy directory beside
them, in both front ends. The comparison facility has four modes — `compare cells`, `repeats`,
`renormalise` and `sweeps` on the command line, and a "Compare saved runs"/"Compare saved sweeps"
group on the two analysis screens — each writing a comparison record. **Execution (2026-09-23/24,
Opus 5.5, subagent-driven):** the pre-flight found that the brief extractor never carries a plan's
preamble and ruled 66 further findings into the tasks; every task had a review that drove the real

a sweep's summary naming no point's own ratio, a delete prompt false for a run writing in another
process, no names for the tool's records or the window's comparisons, and a walkthrough row that

3. ~~GPU gate after piece 1~~ — done 2026-09-11, GREEN at `bb38f1a` (table below). **The first
   attempt at `19363d2` FAILED twice** and both failures were piece-1 regressions on paths no CPU
   suite can reach: (a) infer's PPC met a CUDA observation row and CPU simulated rows —
   `store.load_observation` handed the row back on `cfg.hw.device` and `infer_and_visualize` bound
   it, while `statistics.conditioning_rows` assembles every row on the CPU by contract; (b) the
   forced-mode prior was refused by its own store the moment `build_prior` read it back
   ("prior.pt holds GMM …, not the one the manifest records") — `file_manager.load_mix_dist`
   rebuilt `Categorical(probs=w)`, whose re-normalisation `w / w.sum()` is not a bitwise fixed
   point in float32 and depends on the device's reduction order (the chi prior round-tripped
   exactly on CUDA and not on the CPU; the forced one on neither). Fixes, each with a failing test
   first: `896e8ff` the loader restores the saved weights into `.probs`/`._param` (exact round
   trip; two CPU tests reproduce the refusal); `bb38f1a` the row stays on the CPU and `sample()`
   moves its own copy, pinned by the new gpu-marked `tests/test_gpu_paths.py` (the tiny SBITEST
   chain on `config.detect_device()`, ~12 s; `build_tiny_run(store, hw=None)`).
   **Recipe correction:** run 2 must use the SAME `NUM_RUNS` as run 1 — the simulation identity
   includes `n_runs`, so `NUM_RUNS=2` after a `NUM_RUNS=4` run 1 keys a NEW cache directory,
   rebuilds the Fisher rotation and never resumes (it did exactly that here, silently, exit 0).
   The recipe is four command lines since T19 (`CLAUDE.md`, spec §3.8): `python -m core smoke --chi
   --t-obs 4.5 --bounds …/master.txt --cell …/master_spont.txt --checkpoint --save --store-root
   <scratch>/smoke` (run 1); run 2 is that line WITHOUT `--save`, with `--prior smoke_prior --stages
   prior,posterior --resume require` added (must resume); run 2b is run 2's line WITHOUT `--resume
   require`, with `--num-runs 2` added instead (must EXIT 1 naming `n_runs` — the incident, now
   loud); then the forced-mode run against its own store. Read literally, keeping `--save` on run 2
   would refuse on the taken name `smoke_posterior` instead of resuming — fixed in the fix-round-1
   pass over this recipe (K4), kept in sync with `CLAUDE.md`. The environment-variable recipe is
   gone with `scripts/smoke_train.py`.

       `decorrelate.build_latent_fisher_rotation` re-derives `subs`, `n_fine`, `t_fine` and `n_segs` on
       every call, and its `s()` rebuilds `base_inits.expand(mm, -1).contiguous()` on every simulation.
       They are not constants: all four follow from the row's own `t_scale`, which the rotation's `t_scale`
       arms and each operating point move, and the expansion depends on the ensemble width (`max(4m, 128)`
       or `m`). Caching the four per `t_scale` value and the expansion per width would save the work;
       moving them out of `feats` would simulate the `t_scale` arms on the anchor's grid and silently
       corrupt the rotation. Not done (`core/SBI/decorrelate.py`).
     - *From the handoff's retirement.* Four speed-ups stay open, each gated: (1) running `gen_chi_raw`'s
       1+K probe simulations as one wider call (up to 8× on the dominant cost) waits on the drive
       builder's 4× transient — `forcing.build_nondim_force_tensor` materialises a full (batch, T) time
       tensor and three more, which `pipeline.peak_sim_elements` and `_max_sim_batch` still count as
       `n_ch * n_fine`; (2) strided single-variable solver output (about 23 % of time, 6× peak) must
       reproduce the segment-seam duplication, keep every variable's final state and take the
       predictive check's per-row subsample; (3) binning the predictive check by a step budget instead
       of `PPC_BIN_SIZE` (bins are t_scale-sorted and keyed on their smallest t_scale); (4)
       `SummaryStatistics`'s duplicate `_acf` and `_analytic_bandpass` calls and `chi.peak_freq`'s
       repeated peak search, about 6 minutes of a production run. A fifth, stacking the Fisher's arms,
       was dropped: 15–30 minutes of about 38 hours, at a high risk to its common random numbers
       (`core/SBI/pipeline.py`, `core/forcing.py`, `core/SBI/ppc.py`, `core/SBI/statistics.py`).
     - *From the handoff's retirement.* Two renames were deferred and never made: `core/gui/vt.py`,

     - A tier-1 box whose units file declares no force token fails with a bare `ValueError` at the prior's

     - `probes drive`: a drive harmonic past Nyquist aliases and is not flagged (at detune 1.4 the second
       harmonic's alias lands on the peak itself for a peak near 0.53 × Nyquist; the review put the affected
       cells at a peak above about a quarter of Nyquist) — a fast lab cell's check could miss a sub-harmonic
       caveat (`core/diagnostics/probes.py`).
     - Band's `_NYQUIST_SHARE = 0.9` is a literal separate from training's `0.9 * nyq` (and eight
       user-visible "0.9 x Nyquist" strings): after a change to training's share, band masks at the old share
       until the mask audit flags it (`core/diagnostics/probes.py`, `core/SBI/chi.py`).

       (`tests/test_source_hygiene.py`).
     - Inherited Fisher eigenvalues are not checked against the parent's V (an API-only risk); a narrowing
       child without them records None silently; a non-finite header eigenvalue fails a resume
       (`core/SBI/training_checkpoint.py`, `core/orchestrator.py`).
     - Behaviour items the ledger routed here: the Config tab accepts two typed unit tokens of one
       dimension (`nm um`), and the two orders of such a pair key one cache (the fingerprint sorts the
       tokens) while resolving differently — a validation refusal would remove it
       (`core/gui/panels/inference/config_tab.py`); the cache identity reads the units file when the identity
       is built, not when it was parsed, so an edit between Apply and Train keys the run by the edited file
       (`core/artifacts/identity.py`); with no units fingerprint, `near_miss_siblings` can offer a
       `training-rows/2` cache that differs only in `format` — it refuses and never reuses, and excluding
       `format` as `truncation` is excluded would drop the offer (`core/SBI/training_checkpoint.py`); an
       in-band "not measured" point makes `band_holds` False where `drive_holds` reads None, and
       `_cross_check` does not count a warning under a tag with no committed record as abandoned, so the
       abandoned count under-reports in a run that already fails (`core/diagnostics/probes.py`); the training
       run-total `[chi] masked probes` line is logged before the final save, widening an existing window by
       one call (`core/SBI/pipeline.py`).
     - Wording owed in code: `probes band`'s `--lengths` default says "to this cell's training ceiling", but
       the default stops at the shorter of 60 s (`T_MAX_EXP_S`) and that ceiling (`core/tool/help_defaults.py`,

    (`docs/superpowers/prompts/2026-10-05-code-audit.md`). The owner supplies a PDF of the models and the audit checks
    that the code implements them correctly, starting from
    `docs/superpowers/notes/2026-10-05-where-the-models-math-lives.md` and its reproduced first lead (the whole-number
    time stride; the paragraph at the top). It owns, from 1 October and never done because no source could be edited while the run was live: fail fast when an
    out-of-memory failure leaves `empty_cache` and `mem_get_info` failing; a per-process memory fraction as an
    argument, after a card measurement; `empty_cache` and the graph drop swapped in `_release_device_memory`. It also
    owns the note's part 3 and the triage of the open list above, the unseeded test
    `test_train_and_validate_without_a_loaded_cell` among it (87–248 s; parked there on 30 September).
11. **The retrain, from the window**, after 9 and 10. `docs/guide/retrain.md` is rewritten as the window route by the
    audit chat. The owner's budget of 2026-09-30 (four weeks of the card for the retrain and its certification round) has
    not been revisited; the stopped run spent about 90 h of the card (27 h of generation, 63 h of fit), and whether that
    counts against it is the owner's to say. The measured costs are ~27 h of generation at a 6 GiB ceiling and 63 minutes
    per full-size epoch.

**The GPU gate, as command lines.** This is `CLAUDE.md`'s recipe of record (its Tests section),
copied verbatim; keep the copies identical (the documents review compares them). Run it from the
repository root, with `$S` set to an empty scratch directory. `PRISM_ARTIFACTS` is not needed for
the five `smoke` runs, which touch
only `--store-root`; the diagnostic card runs need it pointed at the same store.

````markdown
  ```powershell
  $py = "C:\Users\J\anaconda3\envs\biophys-env\python.exe"
  $B  = "--bounds","Resources/Bounds/nadrowski/master.txt"
  $C  = "--cell","Resources/Cells/nadrowski/master_spont.txt"
  $S  = "<scratch>"
  # run 1: chi; builds and names smoke_prior/smoke_posterior; writes the simulation cache
  & $py -m core smoke --chi --t-obs 4.5 @B @C --checkpoint --save --store-root "$S/smoke"
  # run 2: same store, same --num-runs; must resume
  & $py -m core smoke --chi --t-obs 4.5 @B @C --checkpoint --store-root "$S/smoke" --prior smoke_prior --stages prior,posterior --resume require
  # run 2b: one setting away; must exit 1 naming n_runs
  & $py -m core smoke --chi --t-obs 4.5 @B @C --checkpoint --store-root "$S/smoke" --prior smoke_prior --stages prior,posterior --num-runs 2
  # run 3: forced mode, its own store
  & $py -m core smoke --no-chi --t-obs 4.5 @B --cell Resources/Cells/nadrowski/master_weak.txt --checkpoint --save --store-root "$S/smoke_chi0"
  # run 4: the tier-1 box (temperature inferred, the force scale derived) at the larger network, its own store
  & $py -m core smoke --chi --t-obs 4.5 --bounds Resources/Bounds/nadrowski/master_tier1.txt --cell Resources/Cells/nadrowski/master_spont_tier1.txt --hidden-features 256 --num-transforms 10 --checkpoint --save --store-root "$S/smoke_t1"
  ```

  Pass criteria, read off `$LASTEXITCODE` and the printed lines after each command:
  - runs 1, 3 and 4: `$LASTEXITCODE` 0, no OOM lines, stage timings near the last recorded card
    run; on the two chi runs (1 and 4) the training `[chi] masked probes` run-total line (the one
    scoped to every committed batch of the simulation cache; the calibration stage logs a second
    one, scoped to this process only, which is not the criterion) within ±12 pp of 37 %;
  - run 2: prints `Reusing the Fisher rotation stored with the training checkpoint` and
    `[checkpoint] resuming at batch 4/4`, `$LASTEXITCODE` 0;
  - run 2b: `$LASTEXITCODE` 1, the refusal names `n_runs`, no `[fisher]` line, no new directory
    under `$S/smoke/simulations/`;
  - run 4, also: both `[tier1]` lines, `rescale order: ['x_scale', 't_scale', 'T']` in the banner,
    and `smoke_posterior`'s `manifest.json` lists `T` in `body.transform.param_keys` and carries a
    `config.tier1` block.

  `--bounds` is required: the same-named sibling rule would otherwise resolve the 12-dim
  spontaneous box. After a change under `core/diagnostics` that moves tensors, also run the
  diagnostic card, each command with `$env:PRISM_ARTIFACTS` set to the store named. Against
  `$S/smoke`: `sbc --chi @B --posterior smoke_posterior --repeats 1 --n-cal 20`,
  `identifiability jacobian --chi @B @C --t-obs 4.5` and `ablation --chi @B --posterior
  smoke_posterior`. Against `$S/smoke_chi0`: `identifiability laplace --no-chi @B --posterior
  smoke_posterior --cell Resources/Cells/nadrowski/master_weak.txt --t-obs 4.5`. Against
  `$S/smoke_t1`: `identifiability jacobian --chi --bounds Resources/Bounds/nadrowski/master_tier1.txt
  --cell Resources/Cells/nadrowski/master_spont_tier1.txt --t-obs 4.5` (its temperature column is a
  measurement, not a failure). Against an empty scratch store: `probes band @B @C` and `probes drive
  @B @C`. Against `$S/smoke`: `probes mask @B --prior smoke_prior`. Each exits 0 and writes one
  record under `diagnostics/`. Delete `$S` afterwards.
````

## Decisions log

- **2026-09-10** — brainstorming: clean break; all four audiences; GUI + one tested CLI tool with
  the prompt CLI retired; reduction map out of scope; everything lands before the retrain;
  pytest adopted; artifact store shape B (directory per artifact + manifest); timestamp ids;
  auto-persist.
- **2026-09-10/11** — piece 1 execution rulings (full text in the ledger): R1 keep the old
  `build_posterior` return for one task; R2 three pre-flight plan fixes; R3 worktree; R4 piece 0's
  gate as baseline; R5 `.gitignore` keeps the `/Resources/*` lines until the runbook; R6
  parameter-keyed results are ordered record lists (manifest JSON sorts keys); R7 the region's
  prior fingerprint is the walk over the parent's pickled prior. The final review's "refuse
  before the spend" principle: a taken name, and a region without an observation digest, are
  refused at stage entry. The recorded fast gate is one process (a split gate hid a
  default-store leak once). The smoke-train artifact names are `smoke_prior`/`smoke_posterior`
  (a leading underscore is not a legal name).
- **2026-09-11** — after the merge: **no more feature branches or worktrees.** Every piece from
  here on is built directly on the local `main` branch in the one checkout; the user pushes.
  (The branch cost a merge, a conflict on a file both sides touched, and a stale copy of this file
  on `main` while it was open.)
- **2026-09-11** — clean break executed (section above). Ruling: archiving into the gitignored
  `archive/` means a move on disk plus `git rm --cached`, never `git mv` — the repository holds no
  tracked-but-ignored file again. `/sbc_run.log` stays in `.gitignore`. A file changed with an
  editor is NOT staged: `git commit` without `git add` commits nothing and says so only in its
  output (the runbook's step 4 needed a second attempt because of it; always check the log).
- **2026-09-11** — GPU gate rulings. (1) A prior's save/load round trip is the IDENTITY, bit for
  bit: `load_mix_dist` restores the saved weights rather than letting `Categorical` re-normalise
  them, because every fingerprint that names a prior hashes those bytes and torch's
  re-normalisation is device-dependent at the last ulp. Every other fingerprint comparison (the
  posterior's pickled training prior, the region, the run guards) is unchanged and now agrees
  across devices for free. (2) A loaded observation row lives on the CPU like every conditioning
  row; the flow's `sample()` is the one consumer that moves a copy. (3) The resume drill's two
  runs share `NUM_RUNS` (the identity includes `n_runs`). (4) GPU-only device errors are the class
  the smoke gate exists for: run it after every piece, before any record run; the gpu-marked
  suite covers the inference path cheaply but is not a substitute (no checkpoint resume, no real
  bounds/cell files).
- **2026-09-11** — **Taskbar icon** (walkthrough row 1, found by the user; fixed in `df7491e`).
  What was seen: the title bar showed the mark, the taskbar button Windows' generic "application"
  glyph — not python's icon either. Ruled out by measurement, each with a launch and a screenshot
  of the button: the PNG set (loads, seven sizes), the AppUserModelID (set and read back; removing
  it or using a fresh id changed nothing), the shell's icon cache, and the multi-size QIcon (a
  minimal window using the very same QIcon got the mark). What decided it: a minimal window idle
  right after showing got the mark; the same window blocked 4 s after showing got the glyph; PRISM's
  `show()` keeps the thread busy ~150 ms AFTER the native show (Qt lays out the whole tree —
  profiled: all inside `QWidget.show`), and the shell's icon query at button creation times out
  and falls back to the window CLASS icon, which Qt registers as the stock IDI_APPLICATION glyph
  because python.exe has no icon resource (the class handle read back equals `LoadIcon(NULL,
  IDI_APPLICATION)`). With the class icon set to ours, the blocked window got the mark too.
  Rulings: (1) the mark is installed as Qt's window-CLASS icon before the first show
  (`app_icon.set_windows_class_icon`, from the new `assets/app/prism.ico` that
  `build_app_icon.py` assembles from the PNG set — LoadImage cannot read a PNG); re-asserting
  `setWindowIcon` on the first loop turn also worked but depends on timing piece 4's start-up work
  could break; (2) the AppUserModelID stays for grouping/pinning and its docstring no longer
  claims it decides the icon; (3) a display-marked test pins the class-icon change on the real
  platform (`QT_QPA_PLATFORM=windows pytest -m display`), an offscreen test pins that prism.ico's
  256 frame IS prism-256.png; (4) verified by screenshot of the fixed launcher's button; the user
  re-checks row 1 on the next launch.
- **2026-09-11/15** — **piece 2 (one flow underneath): decisions D1–D13** (spec
  `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-11-one-flow-design.md` §1.1; plan
  `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-12-one-flow.md`). D1 the prompt CLI is retired WHOLE
  (`orchestrator.run`, `core/app.py`, the `__main__` menu, the prompt half of `core/cli.py`,
  `helpers.clear_screen`'s callers, `run_fdt`'s `input()` calls, whose two booleans are now
  required). D2/D6 the flags-only tool `python -m core <subcommand>`: every flag reaches its stage
  as a keyword argument, `--bounds` is required wherever a prior is built or training runs, and the
  tool reads only `PRISM_RESOURCES` and `PRISM_ARTIFACTS`. D3 three compositions beside the stages
  (`simulated_inference`, `experimental_inference`, `tsnpe_round`), each carrying the checks that
  used to live in one front end only. D4 six scripts became four subcommands (`smoke`, `sbc`,
  `identifiability` with three modes, `ablation`), six were archived, `_common` dissolved. D5 the
  `diagnostic` store kind, whose loader has no refusals. D7 a committed simulation cache ONE
  setting away (a "near miss") is refused before the Fisher step and before any simulation;
  `new_run=True` / `--new-run` overrides it; the resume policy `auto`/`require`/`never` is a stage
  argument and the `--resume` flag; the GUI dialog asks `fresh_run_near_misses`, and the stage
  restates its checks field for field (spec §11 row 2). D8 the tool
  refuses by default and `--accept-*` maps 1:1 onto `Accept`; the Posterior tab asks before it
  loads a stored non-amortized posterior. D9 every driven chi recording states its frequency in Hz;
  the legacy branch is deleted. D10 the entry sets `KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK` and the Agg backend
  before any torch or core import, so `--help` stays torch-free. **D11 there is NO chi override
  anywhere: a non-default band or drive amplitude means editing `config.py` deliberately.** **D12 a
  TSNPE round whose parent is itself non-amortized and whose observation is not that parent's is
  refused, with no escape hatch.** D11 and D12 are STANDING REFUSALS: a later piece must not
  re-open them. D13 `PRISM_VRAM_CEILING_GIB` and `PRISM_MEM_LOG_EVERY` stay core-level environment
  settings, named in the tool's `--help` epilog and in `CLAUDE.md`, not arguments;
  `PRISM_VRAM_CEILING_GIB` is read live on each batch plan, `PRISM_MEM_LOG_EVERY` once, when
  `core.SBI.pipeline` is imported. Spec §1.2's readings stand: `runners.py` is deleted rather than
  wrapped; the one-setting rule still exempts a cache that differs only in its truncation region (a
  TSNPE round at its parent's budget); only `smoke` and the diagnostics take `--seed`; a
  hand-entered truth drops the cell from the recorded sources.
- **2026-09-14/15** — piece 2's working rules. **Folding versus archiving:** a FOLDED script is
  `git rm`'d (git history is its archive, and an archived copy would drift from its replacement);
  an ARCHIVED script is moved on disk into the gitignored `archive/scripts/` and then `git rm
  --cached`, never `git mv` — the same ruling as the clean break's. **Test-side knob defaults:**
  `tests/conftest.py`'s session-scoped autouse `_checkpointing_off_unless_asked` is the only
  session-wide knob default the suites install, and every test that wants a simulation cache
  passes `checkpoint_every` explicitly. **Scan roots:** `CODE_ROOTS` (the top-level directories,
  today `core`) plus `CODE_FILES` (top-level Python files, today `conftest.py`) in
  `tests/_fixtures.py` are what the source scans walk (the literal-path, `input()` and rotation-reader scans); a guard fails if a
  directory holding Python is missing from `CODE_ROOTS`, and a missing `CODE_FILES` entry fails
  with a message naming it. **The Fisher is truth-free on the command line:** `smoke` builds a
  config with no ground truth, so its rotation anchors at the prior median plus seven prior draws
  rather than at the cell's truth as `smoke_train` did. So only the prior and validate timings (and
  the masked-probe share) compare with `bb38f1a`; the posterior and infer timings, `V` and
  `fisher_spread` do not.
- **2026-09-14/15** — piece 2's execution rulings, condensed to the load-bearing ones. The ledger
  (`.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-12-one-flow/progress.md`, compiled in `rulings-and-open-items.md`) is
  gitignored scratch; the durable record is this log plus spec §11.
  - Each task's gate result goes into the ledger, not this file; this table gets only gates of
    record and the final rows (R-C).
  - A plan's predicted counts, error texts and file lists are predictions; what must hold is the
    behaviour: the test fails before the change and passes after (R-A).
  - A leftover-mention search accepts history notes ("Folded from scripts/x.py"); a hit that
    imports, builds a path to, or tells someone to run a retired thing fails it (R-B).
  - Piece 2's commit range starts after `b578f08`; a task whose check changes nothing needs no
    commit (R-H).
  - One GPU recipe text: `CLAUDE.md`'s block, copied verbatim under the gate table (R-I, R-V).
  - Refuse before the spend: `infer` checks its recording flags before loading anything, and
    `infer --cell` with any recording flag exits 2 (R-L); the near-miss refusal sits before the
    truncation refusals and the Fisher step (T2); bad knobs and zero sample counts are refused
    before any simulation (T16–T18, final review).
  - The near-miss refusal message names both GUI controls and `--new-run` (R-O).
  - The diagnostics declare and build their accept flags through the shared `config_args` helpers
    (R-T).
  - Warnings in test output are findings: a leaked warning is captured and asserted; a third-party
    warning from a real run the spec requires is accepted unfiltered and the baseline moves (T4, T6,
    T11, T12).
  - Only inferences and diagnostics record accept flags in `results.accepted`; whether a
    calibration or TSNPE child should is parked for the owner (R-S; "Owed" item 7).
  - `identifiability laplace` isolates only its fixed-seed calls, so its noise-floor draws stay
    independent per point and `--seed` governs every point (T17).
  - The GPU gate of record ran alone on the card at `0d96d2f`, after T19's fix round, and its row
    was committed separately (R-U). It passed although posterior and infer missed "within a few
    seconds of `bb38f1a`": the truth-free Fisher explains the difference, and prior and validate
    match (T19).
  - Walkthrough row B8 was added because `bb22ac4` changed the FDT plots after row 15 last passed
    (T24).
  - End order: whole-branch review, one fix dispatch and a scoped re-review, then the slow set and
    the final fast gate, then these documents from the measured numbers (R-W); the documents
    follow the ledger where the T25 brief was stale (R-X).
  - The final review's six must-fix and 21 fix-now items landed in one dispatch, each behaviour fix
    test-first; 46 items stay open with reasons. Follow-ups: the SBC rank histogram's bin count is
    the largest divisor of `nps + 1` between half the cap and the cap, else the cap; the flow-knob
    range checks run only on a call that trains; the chi probe count left on a GUI session is
    piece 3's.

    `lru_cache` singleton, so the deep copy must share it (row 3); the stage-entry order is the name
    first in the four stages and the knobs first only in the two compositions (row 5); the judgements
    said once are `PreflightWarning`s with the `always` filter (row 7); one refusal still names the
    Settings model builder, because nothing else answers it (row 9); the worker drops the exception's
    traceback so a failed run frees its frames (row 10); warnings under `public_entry` skip
    `core/runs.py` when locating their caller (row 11).
  - Working rules: the plan's quoted code was drafted against `ca583f8` and never verified, so each
    implementer adapted a moved quote and said so; implementers ran only their focused tests in the
    foreground and the controller ran each one-process gate (two implementers stalled waiting on a
